时间
发布日期:2026-08-06;活动期间:截至2026年7月26日的一周(2026年第30周)。
地点与对象
全球,重点关注南半球温带和亚热带地区;中美洲、加勒比海、热带南美洲、南亚、东亚。
事件
世卫组织全球呼吸道病毒活动每周更新第 589 号报告了 2026 年第 30 周的 季节性流感 和 SARS-CoV-2 监测。全球流感阳性率仍低于 10%,南半球温带和亚热带地区流感活动升高(>10%)。全球 SARS-CoV-2 阳性率保持稳定且较低,其中中美洲、加勒比海、热带南美洲以及南亚和东亚的单个国家的阳性率较高(>10%)。
规模、影响与风险
流感:总体阳性率<10%;南半球温带和亚热带地区升高(>10%)。SARS-CoV-2:全球阳性率稳定且较低;中美洲、加勒比海、热带南美洲以及南亚和东亚的单个国家升高(>10%)。世卫组织鼓励使用 GISRS 试剂盒对流感和 SARS-CoV-2 进行综合 监测。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织鼓励拥有 GISRS 多重试剂盒的国家进行流感和 SARS-CoV-2 监测 综合分析,并向区域和全球平台及时报告数据。严重性评估由国家、地区和领地报告。数据源自前哨和系统病毒学 监测,从报告 #501 开始。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Global Respiratory Virus Activity: Weekly Update N° 589 - World Health Organization (WHO)

News Brief
The World Health Organization’s Weekly Update N° 589 (6 August 2026) reports on global respiratory virus activity for week 30, 2026. Globally, influenza positivity remained below 10%, but exceeded 10% in southern hemisphere temperate and subtropical regions. SARS-CoV-2 activity stayed low overall, though positivity surpassed 10% in Central America, the Caribbean, Tropical South America, and single countries in Southern and Eastern Asia. WHO urges countries with GISRS multiplex reagent kits to integrate influenza and SARS-CoV-2 surveillance and submit timely data to regional and global platforms. Severity assessments are provided by reporting countries, areas, and territories, with surveillance data drawn from sentinel and systematic virologic systems introduced in report #501.

reports Governance Governing bodies World Health Assembly Executive Board Member States Portal Home/ Publications/ Overview/ Global Respiratory Virus Activity: Weekly Update N° 589 Global Respiratory Virus Activity: Weekly Update N° 589 Week 30, ending 26 July 2026 6 August 2026 | Emergency situation update Download (5.8 MB) Overview In week 30 2026, influenza positivity remained below 10% and SARS-CoV-2 activity remained low globally. Influenza positivity was above 10% in the southern hemisphere temperate and subtropical areas. RSV positivity also remained low globally. Influenza Globally, influenza detections remained low in week 30 with influenza A and B viruses detected in similar proportions. In the southern hemisphere, influenza percent positivity was elevated (>10%) in some countries in Temperate South America and in single countries in Tropical South America, Eastern Africa and South-East Asia. No increases in activity were observed in the southern hemisphere. In the northern hemisphere, influenza percent positivity was elevated (>10%) in some countries in Central America and the Caribbean, Southern and South-East Asia and in single countries in Middle Africa, Western and Eastern Asia. Percent positivity was over 30% in single countries in Central America and the Caribbean and Tropical South America. Increases in activity were observed in single countries in Central America and the Caribbean, Tropical South America, South West Europe and South-East Asia. In the zones with elevated positivity, influenza A(H3N2) was predominant in Central America and the Caribbean, Tropical South America and Middle Africa and influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 was predominant in Eastern Africa, Western and Southern Asia. Influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 and influenza A(H3N2) were codominant in South-East Asia; and influenza A and B were codominant in Temperate South America and Eastern Asia. SARS-CoV-2 Globally, SARS-CoV-2 positivity remained stable and low across most reporting countries, with elevated positivity (>10%) reported in countries in Central America and the Caribbean and Tropical South America and in single countries in Southern and Eastern Asia. Increases in activity were observed in two countries in Central America and the Caribbean and in single countries in Tropical South America, and Western Africa. Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) RSV positivity was elevated (>10%) in a few countries in Central America and the Caribbean, Tropical and Temperate South America and in a single country in Southern Africa. Percent positivity was over 30% in a single country in Central America and the Caribbean. Small increases in activity were observed in single countries in Central America and the Caribbean and Temperate South America. RSV and influenza activity were both elevated in a few countries in Central America and the Caribbean and Temperate South America. Severity assessment The severity assessments here are reported from countries, areas and territories. Assessments for transmissibility can be reported based on syndromic parameters and/or influenza-specific parameters. In the southern hemisphere temperate and subtropical areas, influenza-specific transmissibility was reported as below seasonal threshold (1); transmissibility using syndromic data was reported as low (1). In the northern hemisphere temperate and subtropical areas, influenza-specific transmissibility was reported as below seasonal threshold (13); transmissibility using syndromic data was reported as below seasonal threshold (10). WHO encourages countries, especially those that have received the multiplex influenza and SARS-CoV-2 reagent kits from GISRS, to conduct integrated surveillance of influenza and SARS-CoV-2 and report epidemiological and laboratory information in a timely manner to established regional and global platforms. The guidance can be found here. Starting with report #501, the Global Respiratory Virus Activity Weekly Update included data from sentinel surveillance and other types of systematically conducted virologic surveillance. Countries, areas, and territories use a variety of approaches to monitor respiratory virus activity and data in this report may vary from surveillance reports posted elsewhere. Analyses stratified by source of surveillance is available through Respimart. WHO Team Epidemic and Pandemic Management (EPM), Global Influenza Surveillance and Response System Editors World Health Organization Number of pages 5

Time
Publication date: 2026-08-06; event period: week ending 26 July 2026 (week 30, 2026).
Location and population
Global, with emphasis on southern hemisphere temperate and subtropical areas; Central America, Caribbean, Tropical South America, Southern Asia, Eastern Asia.
Event
WHO Global Respiratory Virus Activity Weekly Update N° 589 reports on seasonal influenza and SARS-CoV-2 surveillance for week 30, 2026. Influenza positivity remained below 10% globally, with elevated (>10%) influenza activity in southern hemisphere temperate and subtropical regions. SARS-CoV-2 positivity remained stable and low globally, with elevated (>10%) positivity in Central America, Caribbean, Tropical South America, and single countries in Southern and Eastern Asia.
Scale, impact and risk
Influenza: global positivity <10%; elevated (>10%) in southern hemisphere temperate and subtropical areas. SARS-CoV-2: global positivity stable and low; elevated (>10%) in Central America, Caribbean, Tropical South America, and single countries in Southern and Eastern Asia. WHO encourages integrated surveillance of influenza and SARS-CoV-2 using GISRS reagent kits.
Response, status and uncertainty
WHO encourages countries with GISRS multiplex reagent kits to conduct integrated influenza and SARS-CoV-2 surveillance and report data promptly to regional and global platforms. Severity assessments are reported from countries, areas, and territories. Data are derived from sentinel and systematic virologic surveillance starting with report #501.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

MRNA Flu 疫苗 为 季节性流感 带来新优势

MRNA Flu Vaccine Brings a Fresh Edge Against Seasonal Influenza
新闻简报美国食品和药物管理局已批准 mFLUSIVA,这是第一个基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗,用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。由 Moderna 开发的 疫苗 在一项涉及超过 40,700 名参与者的 3 期试验中显示,与传统流感疫苗相比,疫苗 对实验室确诊流感的保护作用提高了 26.6%。与传统的 疫苗 不同,mFLUSIVA 使用 mRNA 技术指导细胞产生流感蛋白,从而能够更快地适应不断变化的病毒株,并可能获得更广泛、更持久的免疫力。虽然酸痛和疲劳等副作用稍微常见一些,但严重的不良事件仍然很少见。FDA 还针对 65 岁及以上的成年人加速批准,具体取决于正在进行的验证性研究以验证长期益处。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-08;所提供的证据中未明确报告事件日期。
地点与对象
美国;50岁及以上的成年人(FDA批准范围)。
事件
FDA 批准了 mFLUSIVA,这是第一个基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗,适用于 50 岁及以上成年人,标志着下一代流感 疫苗 开发的里程碑。
规模、影响与风险
所提供的证据中未报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
基于 3 期 FLUENT 试验(超过 40,700 名参与者)的批准显示,与标准流感疫苗相比,对实验室确诊流感的保护效果提高了 26.6%;表明更广泛的抗体反应和更持久的免疫力;上市后研究正在进行中;加速批准年龄≥65岁的成年人,等待验证性研究。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

MRNA Flu Vaccine Brings a Fresh Edge Against Seasonal Influenza

News Brief
The U.S. Food and Drug Administration has granted approval to mFLUSIVA, the first mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine, for adults aged 50 and older. Developed by Moderna, the vaccine demonstrated 26.6% higher protection against laboratory-confirmed influenza compared to conventional flu shots in a Phase 3 trial involving over 40,700 participants. Unlike traditional vaccines, mFLUSIVA uses mRNA technology to instruct cells to produce influenza proteins, enabling faster adaptation to evolving viral strains and potentially broader, longer-lasting immunity. While side effects such as soreness and fatigue were slightly more common, serious adverse events remained rare. The FDA also issued accelerated approval for adults 65 and older, contingent on ongoing confirmatory studies to verify long-term benefits.
Original source text (English)

MEDINDIA MEDINDIA Consult Online Doctor Courses Explore Healthy Living News Health A-Z Calculators Articles Drugs Directories Education More Search Medindia Medindia » News » Health In Focus » MRNA Flu Vaccine Brings a Fresh Edge Against Seasonal Influenza MRNA Flu Vaccine Brings a Fresh Edge Against Seasonal Influenza Written by Dr. Himanshi Porwal Medically Reviewed by Colleen Fleiss M.Sc on Aug 8 2026 12:14 PM Listen to this article 0:00/0:00 The first mRNA flu vaccine has been approved for adults aged 50 years and older, offering stronger protection against seasonal influenza than a standard flu shot. Highlights: FDA approves the first mRNA seasonal flu vaccine for adults aged 50 years and older The vaccine showed 26.6% higher protection against laboratory-confirmed influenza Researchers say mRNA technology could improve future flu vaccines by adapting faster to changing viruses Seasonal influenza continues to place a heavy burden on healthcare systems worldwide, especially among older adults who face a higher risk of severe illness, hospitalization, and death (1✔ ✔Trusted Source BLA APPROVAL Go to source). Although annual influenza vaccination remains the best way to reduce complications, the virus constantly changes, making it difficult for conventional vaccines to provide consistent protection every season. The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved mFLUSIVA, the first mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine for adults aged 50 years and older. The approval is supported by evidence from a large Phase 3 clinical trial published in the New England Journal of Medicine (NEJM), along with other immunology studies, which suggest that mRNA technology may generate broader and longer-lasting immune responses than conventional influenza vaccines (2✔ ✔Trusted Source Efficacy and Safety of an mRNA Seasonal Influenza Vaccine in Adults Go to source). While additional post-marketing studies are still underway, the approval represents an important milestone in the development of next-generation influenza vaccines. Travel Vaccination Calculator Find out about travel shots or vaccination before you travel to your destination country. Travel vaccination calculator guides travelers on information about travel to 247 countries. Safe travel is the first step to enjoy your holiday or business ... Why Is the First mRNA Flu Vaccine Considered a Major Breakthrough? mFLUSIVA is the first mRNA vaccine approved specifically for the prevention of seasonal influenza in adults aged 50 years and older. Unlike conventional influenza vaccines that are produced using egg- or cell-based manufacturing methods, the new vaccine uses messenger RNA (mRNA) technology to instruct the body's cells to temporarily produce influenza proteins, allowing the immune system to recognize and fight the virus without exposure to the virus itself. Researchers believe this platform offers several potential advantages. Because influenza viruses undergo frequent genetic changes, mRNA vaccines can be updated more rapidly to match newly circulating strains. According to researchers in Nature Immunology, this technology also appears capable of generating broader antibody responses, potentially improving protection against influenza viruses that continue to evolve after the annual vaccine has been developed (2✔ ✔Trusted Source Efficacy and Safety of an mRNA Seasonal Influenza Vaccine in Adults Go to source). The FDA has also granted accelerated approval for adults aged 65 years and older, recognizing the urgent need for better influenza protection in this high-risk group while requiring Moderna to complete confirmatory clinical studies to verify long-term clinical benefit. Travel Vaccination Quiz: Test Your Knowledge Before You Fly Planning a trip abroad Before you pack your bags, test your knowledge with this Travel Vaccination Quiz. Discover which vaccines are exactly required for international travel and what’s recommended by the CDC and WHO, including ... Did the New Vaccine Perform Better Than Standard Flu Vaccines? The approval rests mainly on the Phase 3 FLUENT trial, which studied over 40,700 adults aged 50 and older. People who got the new mRNA flu vaccine caught confirmed flu less often than those who got a standard flu shot, about 26.6% better protection. Side effects like soreness, tiredness, and headache were a bit more common with the mRNA shot, but serious problems were rare and about equal in both groups. In simple terms: better protection, no extra safety risk. Respiratory Diseases - Diseases of the Human Respiratory System Respiratory diseases affect any part of the respiratory system. Some of the lung diseases like chronic obstructive pulmonary disease can lead to respiratory failure. How the New mRNA Flu Vaccine Compares with a Standard Flu Vaccine Parameter New mRNA Flu Vaccine (mFLUSIVA) Standard Flu Vaccine Vaccine technology mRNA-based (same platform used in COVID-19 vaccines) Conventional, grown in eggs or cells Study population 40,700+ adults aged 50 years and above Same trial, comparator group Vaccine efficacy 26.6% higher relative efficacy against lab-confirmed flu Reference (baseline) vaccine Common side effects Injection-site pain, fatigue, headache, muscle aches Similar side effects, but less frequent Duration of mild side effects Typically 1 to 3 days Typically 1 to 3 days Serious adverse events Rare Rare, similar rate Overall safety profile No new safety concerns identified Well-established, long-standing safety record Key takeaway Stronger protection with a comparable safety profile Standard, reliable protection Flu Vaccine Protects Kids from Severe Illness The influenza (flu) vaccine offers the highest protection for kids aged 6 months to 8 years, reducing their risk of severe illness. Why Could mRNA Technology Offer Broader and Longer-Lasting Protection? Beyond the positive Phase 3 results, earlier studies suggest that mRNA technology may produce a stronger and more durable immune response than conventional influenza vaccines. According to Nature Immunology, the investigational mRNA influenza vaccine was associated with: Higher antibody levels against influenza viruses. More memory B cells, which help the body respond quickly to future infections. Broader antibody responses capable of recognizing a wider range of influenza variants. Persistent germinal center activity for up to 26 weeks in some participants, allowing antibodies to continue maturing and diversify over time. Supporting these findings, researchers in npj Vaccines reported that the investigational vaccine (4✔ ✔Trusted Source An mRNA influenza vaccine induces immunity comparable to an adjuvanted vaccine in a randomized trial Go to source): Generated durable antibody responses lasting up to one year. Maintained strong memory B-cell and T-cell responses. Demonstrated an acceptable safety profile in early clinical studies. Together, these findings suggest that mRNA technology could provide broader and longer-lasting protection against influenza while supporting the development of next-generation flu vaccines. How Could mRNA Technology Improve Future Flu Vaccines? Influenza viruses change constantly, making it difficult for seasonal vaccines to provide consistent protection every year. mRNA technology may overcome some of the challenges by generating broader, longer-lasting immune responses and adapting more quickly to circulating virus strains. Key findings from both studies are summarized below. Potential Advantages of mRNA Technology for Influenza Vaccines Current Challenge How mRNA Technology May Help Influenza viruses evolve rapidly Vaccine can be updated more quickly to match circulating strains. Limited protection against new variants Produced broader antibody responses capable of recognizing diverse influenza strains. Immunity may weaken over time Generated durable antibody, memory B-cell, and T-cell responses. Antibodies lose effectiveness as viruses mutate Stimulated persistent germinal center activity for up to 26 weeks, allowing antibodies to continue maturing and diversify. Need for better long-term protection Expanded antibody diversity may improve protection against evolving influenza viruses and support future universal vaccine development. Why Are Better Flu Vaccines Still Needed? Seasonal influenza vaccines remain the best way to reduce serious illness and hospitalization. However, according to JAMA Network Open, influenza viruses continue to evolve, making it difficult for vaccines to provide consistent protection every year. The 2025–2026 influenza season highlighted several ongoing challenges (5✔ ✔Trusted Source Influenza Activity and Estimated Vaccine Effectiveness During the 2025-2026 Influenza Season Go to source): Influenza A(H3N2) viruses caused most infections during the season. Seasonal flu vaccines continued to reduce emergency department visits and hospitalizations, confirming their important public health role. Vaccine effectiveness declined when circulating viruses drifted away from the strains included in the vaccine, reducing protection in some people. Researchers suggest that mRNA technology could help overcome this challenge because vaccine strains can be updated more rapidly than conventional influenza vaccines, potentially improving the match with circulating viruses. Frequently Asked Questions Q: What is mFLUSIVA and what type of Flu Vaccine is it? A: mFLUSIVA is an FDA-approved mRNA influenza vaccine for adults aged 50 years and older. It uses messenger RNA (mRNA) technology to trigger an immune response against seasonal influenza. Q: How does the mFLUSIVA mRNA Flu Vaccine Work? A: The mFLUSIVA mRNA flu vaccine uses messenger RNA to instruct cells to produce influenza proteins that stimulate an immune response. Unlike traditional flu vaccines, it does not use the influenza virus itself to generate immunity. Q: How effective is the mFLUSIVA mRNA Influenza Vaccine? A: In a Phase 3 clinical trial involving more than 40,700 adults, mFLUSIVA demonstrated 26.6% higher protection against laboratory-confirmed influenza compared with a standard-dose flu vaccine. Q: Is mFLUSIVA Safe for Adults? A: The Phase 3 clinical trial identified no new safety concerns with mFLUSIVA. Common side effects included injection-site pain and fatigue, while serious adverse events were uncommon. Q: Who is Eligible to Receive the mFLUSIVA Flu Vaccine? A: mFLUSIVA is FDA-approved for adults aged 50 years and older. The vaccine received accelerated approval for adults aged 65 years and above, subject to the applicable regulatory requirements. Q: Why are mRNA Influenza Vaccines Needed? A: Seasonal influenza viruses continually evolve, which can reduce the effectiveness of conventional flu vaccines when circulating strains differ from vaccine strains. mRNA influenza vaccines could offer a flexible approach to developing and updating vaccines against changing flu viruses. Q: What makes mFLUSIVA Different from Traditional Flu Vaccines? A: The key difference is the vaccine technology. mFLUSIVA uses mRNA to stimulate an immune response, while conventional influenza vaccines typically use inactivated or otherwise prepared viral components. This makes mFLUSIVA part of a newer generation of influenza vaccine technology. B routine priorityPublic-health news

FDA approves Moderna's mRNA seasonal flu vaccine - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
On August 5, 2026, the U.S. Food and Drug Administration approved Moderna’s mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine, mFLUSIVA, for adults aged 50 and older, marking the first such approval in the United States. The decision follows a clinical trial in adults aged 50–64 that showed the vaccine reduced confirmed flu cases by 27% compared with the standard vaccine, with a comparable safety profile though more mild, transient side effects. Moderna will now gather additional data during the 2026–2027 flu season to evaluate performance against the high-dose vaccine currently recommended for those 65 and older, with potential FDA reassessment pending these results. The vaccine is expected to be available ahead of the 2026–2027 respiratory illness season.

The vaccine, mFLUSIVA, is expected to be available for people 50 and older for the 2026-2027 respiratory illness season. "Flu remains a significant public health challenge, and mFLUSIVA provides an important new option for America's seniors. This approval also reflects the ongoing potential of our mRNA platform to help address important public health challenges through continued scientific innovation," Stéphane Bancel, Chief Executive Officer of Moderna, said in a statement. Elise Amendola/AP, FILE In a clinical trial for people aged 50 to 64, the mRNA vaccine led to 27% fewer confirmed flu cases than the current standard vaccine. The safety profile for Moderna’s vaccine and the current standard was about the same, but more people taking the mRNA vaccine reported side effects; those were mostly mild and lasted two days or less. For people 65 and older, a high-dose vaccine is currently recommended. Moderna said it will keep data this winter on how mFlusiva does compared to the current high-dose vaccine and submit that to the FDA to see if the vaccine remains approved for that population. Earlier this year, the FDA announced it would not review Moderna's application for the vaccine. However, the agency backtracked and agreed to review the shot after Moderna promised to conduct another study after the shot became available on the market. Kinney Drugs opens seasonal flu vaccine appointments - AOL.com

News Brief
Gene Morse Updated Tue, August 4, 2026 at 3:38 PM UTC 0 GOUVERNEUR, N.Y. (WWTI) – Kinney Drugs has opened seasonal flu vaccination appointments in all its pharmacies. Kinney Drugs has vaccines on hand, available for those ages 2-64 in New York; 3-64 in Vermont; and High-dose vaccines for seniors age 65+. For the 2026-2027 season, flu vaccines are a trivalent formulation offering protection against three flu viruses: A/H1N1; A/H3N2; and B/Victoria Lineage. The new H3N2 component was selected to better address a subclade of the virus that was predominant during the 25/26 season. Influenza vaccines are reviewed and reformulated annually to address currently circulating strains of the virus. Appointments can be scheduled at the Kinney Drugs website or by using the Kinney Drugs app.
Original source text (English)
Gene Morse Updated Tue, August 4, 2026 at 3:38 PM UTC 0 GOUVERNEUR, N.Y. (WWTI) – Kinney Drugs has opened seasonal flu vaccination appointments in all its pharmacies. Kinney Drugs has vaccines on hand, available for those ages 2-64 in New York; 3-64 in Vermont; and High-dose vaccines for seniors age 65+. For the 2026-2027 season, flu vaccines are a trivalent formulation offering protection against three flu viruses: A/H1N1; A/H3N2; and B/Victoria Lineage. The new H3N2 component was selected to better address a subclade of the virus that was predominant during the 25/26 season. Influenza vaccines are reviewed and reformulated annually to address currently circulating strains of the virus. Appointments can be scheduled at the Kinney Drugs website or by using the Kinney Drugs app. When booking online: Find the “ Pharmacy ” tab; Click to “ Vaccines and Immunizations ”; and Select from the menu. A list of stores is available on the website, or you may search by zip code. Walk-in appointments are also available at neighborhood locations. The 2026-2027 School Year Immunization Requirements for NYS can be found HERE . “ Earlier is better when it comes to getting protected against illness ,” said John Marraffa, R.Ph., President of Kinney Drugs. “ Kinney is now ready to address the upcoming flu season with plenty of vaccine supply and easy, comfortable options to help our patients and communities stay healthy .” Anyone can get sick; however, research shows that some populations are at a higher risk for developing serious complications. “ Serious illness can happen to anyone, but some populations are at higher risk ,” Marraffa said. “ Older adults, people with chronic conditions like asthma, diabetes, or heart disease, those who are pregnant, and young children can all experience more severe flu outcomes. Getting vaccinated every year is important, to protect yourself and the people you care about .” You can learn more by visiting their website , downloading the new Kinney Drugs app, or speaking with their local Kinney Drugs pharmacy team. 美国批准首个基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗

US approves first mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine
新闻简报美国食品和药物管理局 (FDA) 已批准 Moderna 的 mFlusiva 疫苗,这是第一个基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗,用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。该批准包括针对 50-64 岁人群的传统批准以及针对 65 岁及以上人群的加速批准(基于 免疫应答 的有力证据)。疫苗 在一项涉及 40,000 多名成年人的后期临床试验中进行了测试,结果表明,与标准剂量流感 疫苗 相比,它在预防实验室确诊的流感样疾病方面的效果提高了 26.6%。另一项对约 3,000 名 65 岁及以上成年人进行的研究发现,与高剂量 疫苗 相比,针对测试的流感病毒株的抗体反应更强。大多数不良反应为轻度或中度,疫苗 组和对照组报告的严重事件发生率相似。Moderna 需要进行进一步的研究,以确认其对老年群体的临床益处,持续的批准可能取决于这些发现。疫苗 预计将在 2026-2027 年呼吸道病毒季节期间上市。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The FDA approved the vaccine on 06 August 2026. The trial data was collected during the 2026-2027 respiratory virus season.
地点与对象
该批准适用于美国,特别针对 50 岁及以上的成年人。
事件
FDA 批准 Moderna 的 mFlusiva 疫苗 用于 50 岁及以上成年人,这标志着美国第一个基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗。该批准基于显示其有效性和安全性的临床试验和研究。
规模、影响与风险
研究发现,疫苗 在预防实验室确诊的流感样疾病方面比标准剂量流感 疫苗 的效果高 26.6%。与高剂量 疫苗 相比,它还在老年人中产生了更强的抗体反应。大多数不良反应为轻度或中度,疫苗 组和对照组报告的严重不良事件和死亡发生率相似。疫苗接种 治疗后 42 天内未发现心肌炎或心包炎病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
Moderna 需要进行一项额外的研究,以确认其对 65 岁及以上成年人的临床益处。批准取决于这项研究的结果。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

US approves first mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine

News Brief
The US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved Moderna’s mFlusiva vaccine, the first mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine, for adults aged 50 and older. The approval includes traditional approval for those aged 50-64 and accelerated approval for those aged 65 and older, based on evidence of a strong immune response. The vaccine was tested in a late-stage clinical trial involving over 40,000 adults, showing it to be 26.6% more effective at preventing laboratory-confirmed influenza-like illness than a standard-dose flu vaccine. A separate study with about 3,000 adults aged 65 and older found stronger antibody responses against tested influenza strains compared to high-dose vaccines. Most adverse reactions were mild or moderate, and serious events were reported at similar rates in both vaccine and comparison groups. Moderna is required to conduct further studies to confirm its clinical benefits for the older age group, with continued approval potentially dependent on these findings. The vaccine is expected to be available during the 2026-2027 respiratory virus season.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content WORLD TÜRKİYE ECONOMY POLITICS ENERGY TECHNOLOGY US-Iran war Hormuz crisis Russia-Ukraine war EDITION Türkçe English BHSC Pусский Français العربية Kurdî کوردی Shqip فارسی македонски Bahasa Indonesia Español HealthAmericas US approves first mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine Moderna’s mFlusiva vaccine approved for those 50, older Dilara Hamit 06 August 2026•Update: 06 August 2026 ANKARA The Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved Moderna’s mFlusiva vaccine for adults aged 50 and older, making it the US’ first approved seasonal influenza vaccine based on messenger RNA technology. The vaccine received traditional approval for adults between 50 and 64. and accelerated approval for those 65 and older, Moderna announced Wednesday. The accelerated approval for the older age group was based on evidence that mFlusiva generated a strong immune response. Moderna is required to conduct an additional study to confirm its clinical benefits for those 65 and older, with continued approval potentially depending on the findings. “Flu remains a significant public health challenge, and mFLUSIVA provides an important new option for America’s seniors,” said Moderna Chief Executive Officer Stephane Bancel. The approval was supported by a late-stage clinical trial involving more than 40,000 adults aged 50 and older. The trial found that mFlusiva was 26.6% more effective at preventing laboratory-confirmed influenza-like illness than a licensed standard-dose flu vaccine. A separate study involving about 3,000 adults aged 65 and older found that mFlusiva produced stronger antibody responses against the tested influenza strains than a high-dose flu vaccine. Reactions such as pain at the injection site, fatigue, headaches and muscle pain were more common among mFlusiva recipients than among those who received comparison vaccines, according to the FDA’s review. Most reactions were mild or moderate and lasted about two days. Serious adverse events and deaths were reported at similar rates in the vaccine and comparison groups, while no cases of myocarditis or pericarditis were identified within 42 days of vaccination, said the review. Unlike most conventional flu vaccines, which are produced using eggs, mFlusiva uses mRNA to instruct the body to produce influenza antigens and trigger an immune response. The technology could allow vaccine manufacturers to update flu shots more quickly to better match circulating strains. Moderna said it expects mFlusiva to become available to eligible adults in the US during the 2026-2027 respiratory virus season. Applications are also under review in the EU, Canada and Australia. The FDA approved the vaccine on 06 August 2026. The trial data was collected during the 2026-2027 respiratory virus season.
Location and population
The approval is for use in the United States, specifically targeting adults aged 50 and older.
Event
The FDA approved Moderna’s mFlusiva vaccine for adults aged 50 and older, marking the first mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine in the US. The approval was based on clinical trials and studies showing its effectiveness and safety.
Scale, impact and risk
The vaccine was found to be 26.6% more effective at preventing laboratory-confirmed influenza-like illness than a standard-dose flu vaccine. It also produced stronger antibody responses in older adults compared to high-dose vaccines. Most adverse reactions were mild or moderate, and serious adverse events and deaths were reported at similar rates in both vaccine and comparison groups. No cases of myocarditis or pericarditis were identified within 42 days of vaccination.
Response, status and uncertainty
Moderna is required to conduct an additional study to confirm its clinical benefits for adults aged 65 and older. The approval is conditional on the findings of this study.

News Brief
The FDA has approved the first mRNA vaccine for seasonal influenza, as reported by Live Science on August 6, 2026. This marks a significant development in the prevention of seasonal flu, which includes strains such as A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B/Victoria and B/Yamagata lineages. The approval is based on rigorous testing and is intended for use in the general population. However, the evidence provided does not include specific details on the number of cases, deaths, or exposures related to this vaccine. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

随着 FDA 批准首例季节性流感,mRNA 革命超越了新冠病毒 疫苗

The mRNA Revolution Moves Beyond COVID as FDA Clears First Seasonal Flu Vaccine
新闻简报FDA 已批准第一个基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗,即 Moderna 的 mFlusiva,用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。此次批准标志着 mRNA 技术的一个重要里程碑,随着流感病毒的进化,该技术可以实现更快的 疫苗 开发和更新。该决定基于一项涉及 40,000 多名参与者的后期临床试验,结果显示 疫苗 比标准剂量流感 疫苗 的有效性提高 26.6%。然而,FDA 加速批准了 65 岁及以上老年人的用药,要求 Moderna 进行进一步研究以确认临床益处。疫苗 预计很快将在部分美国零售商处上市,但广泛的商业应用可能会推迟到 2027 年流感季节。医疗保险预计将为符合条件的受益人承保 疫苗,而年轻人的承保范围可能取决于个人健康计划和州政策。此次批准凸显了 mRNA 技术比传统方法更有效地解决公共卫生挑战的潜力。
查看新闻五要素
时间
FDA 于 2026 年 8 月 6 日批准了第一个基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗。该批准基于一项涉及 40,000 多名 50 岁及以上成年人的后期临床试验。
地点与对象
疫苗 适合美国 50 岁及以上的成年人。预计未来几周内将在部分美国零售商处发售。
事件
FDA 批准了 Moderna 的 mFlusiva 用于 50 岁及以上成年人,这标志着第一个基于 mRNA 的季节性流感 疫苗。该批准包括对 65 岁及以上成年人的加速授权,要求 Moderna 完成上市后研究以确认临床益处。
规模、影响与风险
研究发现,疫苗 在预防流感方面比许可的标准剂量流感 疫苗 有效 26.6%。它还在 65 岁及以上的成年人中产生了比赛诺菲高剂量流感 疫苗 更强的抗体反应。由于需要额外的证据,FDA 加快了对老年人的批准。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 根据临床试验数据批准了 疫苗,但对于 65 岁及以上的成年人仍需要额外的证据。医疗保险预计将为符合条件的受益人承保 疫苗,而年轻人的保险承保范围可能会有所不同。此次批准是 mRNA 技术的一个里程碑,可能会改变未来流感 疫苗 的开发和更新方式。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

The mRNA Revolution Moves Beyond COVID as FDA Clears First Seasonal Flu Vaccine

News Brief
The FDA has approved the first mRNA-based seasonal flu vaccine, Moderna's mFlusiva, for adults aged 50 and older. This approval marks a significant milestone for mRNA technology, which could allow for faster vaccine development and updates as influenza viruses evolve. The decision was based on a late-stage clinical trial involving over 40,000 participants, showing the vaccine to be 26.6% more effective than standard-dose flu vaccines. However, the FDA granted accelerated approval for seniors aged 65 and older, requiring Moderna to conduct further studies to confirm clinical benefits. The vaccine is expected to be available at select U.S. retailers soon, though widespread commercial uptake may be delayed until the 2027 flu season. Medicare is anticipated to cover the vaccine for eligible beneficiaries, while coverage for younger adults may depend on individual health plans and state policies. This approval highlights the potential of mRNA technology to address public health challenges more efficiently than traditional methods.
Original source text (English)
International Business Times, Singapore Edition Asia Pacific US Technology Business Fintech Sports Entertainment Science Celebsway Lifestyle Science Health The mRNA Revolution Moves Beyond COVID as FDA Clears First Seasonal Flu Vaccine The approval includes accelerated authorization for adults 65 and older while Moderna completes an additional study to confirm clinical benefit. By Jeanna Francis August 6, 2026 19:27 +08 Moderna Headquarters in Cambridge, Massachusetts. Wikimedia Commons The mRNA technology that reshaped the global response to COVID-19 has officially entered routine seasonal healthcare. The U.S. Food and Drug Administration on Tuesday approved the first mRNA-based influenza vaccine, clearing Moderna's mFlusiva for adults aged 50 and older in a milestone that could change how flu vaccines are developed and updated in the years ahead. Researchers say the technology could allow manufacturers to update flu vaccines more quickly as influenza viruses change, potentially improving protection against circulating strains. Unlike most traditional flu vaccines, which are produced using eggs and contain viral proteins, mFlusiva uses messenger RNA (mRNA) technology. The vaccine instructs the body's cells to produce influenza antigens, triggering an immune response without exposing people to the flu virus itself. Scientists say the platform can be updated more rapidly than conventional vaccine manufacturing if circulating flu strains change. What evidence led to the approval? The FDA based its decision on a late-stage clinical trial involving more than 40,000 adults aged 50 and older. FDA Center for Food Safety and Applied Nutrition in College Park, MD Wikimedia Commons According to Moderna, the study found mFlusiva was 26.6% more effective than a licensed standard-dose flu vaccine in preventing influenza. The company also submitted separate Phase 3 data showing the vaccine generated stronger antibody responses than Sanofi's high-dose flu vaccine in adults aged 65 and older. Because additional evidence is still needed for seniors, the FDA granted accelerated approval for adults 65 and older, requiring Moderna to complete a post-marketing study to confirm the vaccine's clinical benefit. Who can receive the vaccine? The newly approved vaccine is intended for adults aged 50 years and older. According to Moderna, mFlusiva is expected to become available at select U.S. retailers in the coming weeks, although analysts say widespread commercial uptake is unlikely until the 2027 flu season because the company missed contracting deadlines for the 2026 season. Medicare is expected to cover the vaccine for eligible beneficiaries, while insurance coverage for younger adults may vary depending on health plans and state policies, according to health policy experts cited by The Washington Post. Why is this approval important? The approval gives Moderna another commercial product beyond its COVID-19 vaccine and represents an important milestone for mRNA technology. The company has been relying on its flu vaccine and a future combined COVID-19 and influenza vaccine to help offset declining COVID vaccine sales. Analysts at Jefferies have previously estimated that Moderna's flu vaccine and combination shot could generate about $750 million in U.S. sales by 2030. The decision also comes after months of regulatory uncertainty. Moderna had previously withdrawn its application for a combined COVID-flu vaccine after the FDA requested additional evidence on the flu component. The company's standalone flu vaccine also faced increased scrutiny earlier this year before eventually receiving approval. What Moderna says "Flu remains a significant public health challenge, and mFLUSIVA provides an important new option for America's seniors," Moderna said in a statement announcing the approval. Moderna Chief Executive Stéphane Bancel added in a company release quoted by The Washington Post: "This approval also reflects the ongoing potential of our mRNA platform to help address important public health challenges through continued scientific innovation." Experts see faster vaccine updates as a key advantage Medical experts have long said mRNA technology could make seasonal flu vaccines easier to update because they can be redesigned more quickly than traditional egg-based vaccines. Before the FDA's decision, former FDA chief scientist Dr. Jesse Goodman told The Washington Post he would "strongly consider" the vaccine for adults aged 50 to 64 because of its higher effectiveness compared with standard-dose flu vaccines. For adults aged 65 and older, he said it could be considered as an alternative option while additional data are collected. Today we announced that the U.S. Food and Drug Administration has approved mFLUSIVAÂ® (mRNA-1010), a new vaccine against seasonal influenza, for use in all adults 50 years and older. Location and population
The vaccine is intended for adults aged 50 years and older in the United States. It is expected to be available at select U.S. retailers in the coming weeks.
Event

Scale, impact and risk
The vaccine was found to be 26.6% more effective than a licensed standard-dose flu vaccine in preventing influenza. It also generated stronger antibody responses than Sanofi's high-dose flu vaccine in adults aged 65 and older. The FDA granted accelerated approval for seniors due to the need for additional evidence.

FDA 批准 Moderna 的 mRNA 季节性流感 疫苗 - BioSpectrum Asia

FDA approves Moderna’s mRNA seasonal influenza vaccine - BioSpectrum Asia
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 6 日,美国食品和药物管理局批准 Moderna 的基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗、mFlusiva 用于 50 岁及以上成年人,这是美国首次获得此类批准。监管机构对 50 至 64 岁的人群进行传统批准,并针对 65 岁及以上的人群进行加速批准。批准得到了一项超过 40,000 名参与者参与的后期试验的支持,该试验表明 mFlusiva 的相对疗效比标准剂量比较药高 26.6%,并且比获得许可的高剂量 疫苗 引发更强的抗体反应。Moderna 现在必须进行验证性研究并提交额外数据以验证对老年人的益处。疫苗 使用 mRNA 指导细胞产生流感抗原,与基于鸡蛋的平台相比,能够更快地更新以匹配流行毒株。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

FDA approves Moderna’s mRNA seasonal influenza vaccine - BioSpectrum Asia

News Brief
On 6 August 2026, the US Food and Drug Administration granted approval to Moderna’s mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine, mFlusiva, for adults aged 50 and older—the first such approval in the United States. The regulator issued traditional approval for ages 50–64 and accelerated approval for those 65 and older. Approval was supported by a late-stage trial of over 40,000 participants, which showed mFlusiva achieved 26.6% higher relative efficacy than a standard-dose comparator and elicited stronger antibody responses than a licensed high-dose vaccine. Moderna must now conduct a confirmatory study and submit additional data to verify benefits in older adults. The vaccine uses mRNA to direct cells to produce influenza antigens, enabling faster updates to match circulating strains compared with egg-based platforms.
Original source text (English)
The US Food and Drug Administration has approved Moderna’s mFlusiva seasonal influenza vaccine for adults aged 50 and older, marking the first US approval of an mRNA-based vaccine for seasonal flu. The regulator granted traditional approval for adults aged 50 to 64 and accelerated approval for people aged 65 and older. Moderna will conduct an additional study and submit confirmatory data to verify the vaccine’s benefit in the older population. The approval was supported by a late-stage clinical trial involving more than 40,000 adults aged 50 and older. MFlusiva demonstrated 26.6% higher relative efficacy than a licensed standard-dose influenza vaccine. The company also submitted data from a separate late-stage study in adults aged 65 and older. The vaccine generated stronger antibody responses than a licensed high-dose influenza vaccine. MFlusiva uses messenger RNA to instruct cells to produce influenza antigens that trigger an immune response. Unlike conventional influenza vaccines that are commonly produced using eggs, the mRNA platform may allow vaccine compositions to be updated more rapidly as circulating strains change. The product will compete with established influenza vaccines supplied by Sanofi, GSK, CSL Seqirus and AstraZeneca. The approval expands Moderna’s commercial vaccine portfolio beyond COVID-19 and establishes a regulatory pathway for further development of its mRNA-based respiratory vaccine programmes. 超越新冠病毒:Moderna 的季节性流感 疫苗 获得 FDA 批准 - Benzinga

Beyond COVID: Moderna Wins FDA Approval for Its Seasonal Flu Vaccine - Benzinga
新闻简报美国食品和药物管理局已授予 Moderna 的 mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010) 标准批准,这是一种基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗,适用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。该决定得到了 FDA 咨询委员会的一致支持,并得到了第 3 阶段数据的支持,该数据显示了针对实验室确诊的流感样疾病的可接受的安全性和有效性。对于 65 岁及以上的成年人,该机构根据免疫原性数据发布了加速批准,并需要进行上市后试验来确认临床益处。Moderna 计划在 2026-2027 年流感季节之前在美国推出 疫苗,并正在欧盟、加拿大和澳大利亚寻求监管审查。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-06;FDA 批准于 2026 年 8 月 6 日宣布。
地点与对象
美国;50 岁及以上符合资格的成年人。
事件
FDA 批准了 Moderna 的 mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010),一种基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗,用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。此次批准是根据 FDA 的 疫苗 和相关生物制品咨询委员会 (VRBPAC) 的一致建议进行的。
规模、影响与风险
Not applicable to an approval event; no case counts, deaths, or exposures reported. The vaccine targets seasonal influenza A and B strains. Safety profile was acceptable in Phase 3 trials with no new safety concerns identified.
应对、状态与不确定性
基于 3 期试验 (n=40,805) 的标准批准,适用于 50-64 岁人群,该试验证明了安全性、反应原性以及对实验室确诊的流感样疾病的相对功效。基于测量高剂量比较剂免疫原性的 3 期研究 (n=2,992),加速批准 65 岁以上人群;需要进行上市后试验来确认临床益处。2026-2027 年呼吸道病毒季节的可用性。正在接受欧盟、加拿大和澳大利亚的监管审查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Beyond COVID: Moderna Wins FDA Approval for Its Seasonal Flu Vaccine - Benzinga

News Brief
The U.S. Food and Drug Administration has granted standard approval to Moderna’s mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010), an mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine, for adults aged 50 and older. The decision follows unanimous backing from the FDA’s advisory committee and is supported by Phase 3 data showing acceptable safety and efficacy against laboratory-confirmed influenza-like illness. For adults 65 and older, the agency issued accelerated approval based on immunogenicity data, with a postmarketing trial required to confirm clinical benefit. Moderna plans to launch the vaccine in the U.S. ahead of the 2026–2027 flu season and is pursuing regulatory reviews in the European Union, Canada, and Australia.
Original source text (English)
The U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) on Wednesday approved Moderna Inc.'s (NASDAQ:MRNA) mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010), a new vaccine against seasonal influenza, for use in all adults 50 years and older. The approval follows unanimous recommendations from the FDA's Vaccines and Related Biological Products Advisory Committee (VRBPAC) supporting mFLUSIVA for adults 50 years of age and older. Phase 3 Trials Validate Efficacy The standard approval for adults between the ages of 50 and 64 stems from an active-controlled, Phase 3 trial that enrolled 40,805 participants. Read Also: Moderna Burns Cash, Suffers Fresh Vaccine Setback Researchers primarily evaluated the vaccine's safety, reactogenicity, and relative efficacy against a standard-dose comparator in preventing laboratory-confirmed influenza-like illnesses caused by either A or B strains. Accelerated FDA Clearance For Seniors For adults 65 years and older, the agency granted accelerated approval based on a separate Phase 3 study involving 2,992 U.S. seniors. The specific trial measured mFLUSIVA's immunogenicity against a high-dose inactivated flu vaccine. While broader analyses support its effectiveness, a postmarketing trial will be conducted to confirm the clinical benefit for this older population. Throughout the Phase 3 program, the vaccine maintained an acceptable safety profile consistent with previous studies, identifying no new safety concerns. Future Market Rollout And Global Expansion Moderna anticipates making mFLUSIVA available to eligible U.S. patients in time for the 2026-2027 respiratory virus season. The flu shot will be offered alongside the company's existing respiratory lineup, including Spikevax, mRESVIA, and mNEXSPIKE. Internationally, the vaccine is currently under regulatory review in the European Union, Canada, and Australia, with additional international submissions scheduled throughout 2026. On Tuesday, Moderna initiated a Phase 1 trial evaluating mRNA-1469, an investigational vaccine designed to prevent Bundibugyo ebolavirus disease, for which no licensed vaccines currently exist. The study is being conducted in Canada as part of a collaboration with the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI), which has committed up to $50 million to support preclinical testing and the Phase 1 trial. MRNA Price Action: Moderna shares were down 1.86% at $55.21 at the time of publication on Thursday, according to Benzinga Pro data. View five news elements
Time
Published on 2026-08-06; FDA approval announced on 2026-08-06.
Location and population
United States; eligible adults aged 50 years and older.
Event
FDA approved Moderna's mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010), an mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine, for adults aged 50 and older. The approval follows unanimous recommendations from the FDA's Vaccines and Related Biological Products Advisory Committee (VRBPAC).
Scale, impact and risk
Not applicable to an approval event; no case counts, deaths, or exposures reported. The vaccine targets seasonal influenza A and B strains. Safety profile was acceptable in Phase 3 trials with no new safety concerns identified.
Response, status and uncertainty
Standard approval for ages 50–64 based on a Phase 3 trial (n=40,805) demonstrating safety, reactogenicity, and relative efficacy against laboratory-confirmed influenza-like illness. Accelerated approval for ages 65+ based on a Phase 3 study (n=2,992) measuring immunogenicity against a high-dose comparator; postmarketing trial required to confirm clinical benefit. availability for the 2026–2027 respiratory virus season. Under regulatory review in the EU, Canada, and Australia.

Moderna 首款基于 mRNA 的流感疫苗获得 FDA 批准

Moderna wins FDA approval for first mRNA-based flu shot
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 6 日,美国食品和药物管理局 (FDA) 批准了 Moderna 首款基于 mRNA 的流感 疫苗,mFlusiva,用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。这一决定是经过长时间的监管审查和独立咨询委员会于 2026 年 6 月的一致建议而做出的。疫苗 以传统批准的方式批准用于 50 至 64 岁的成年人,以加速批准的方式批准用于 65 岁及以上的成年人,需要进一步的研究来确认其在老年人群中的临床益处。此次批准基于一项涉及 40,000 多名参与者的后期临床试验的结果,其中 疫苗 证明与标准剂量季节性流感 疫苗 相比,疫苗 对抗流感的有效性提高了 26.6%。此外,它在老年人中表现出比赛诺菲高剂量流感 疫苗 更强的 免疫应答。试验期间没有发现重大安全问题,但由于错过了合同周期,Moderna 预计要到 2027 年底才能产生可​​观的收入。此次批准将 Moderna 的产品组合扩展到了 COVID-19 疫苗 之外,并支持其开发基于 mRNA 的 疫苗 用于多种传染病的战略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
美国食品和药物管理局于 2026 年 8 月 6 日批准了 Moderna 的基于 mRNA 的流感 疫苗。该决定是在经过漫长的监管审查以及独立咨询委员会于 2026 年 6 月的一致建议后做出的。
地点与对象
该批准在美国有效,专门针对 50 岁及以上的成年人,针对 50 至 64 岁和 65 岁及以上的成年人有不同的批准类别。
事件
FDA 批准 Moderna 的基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗、mFlusiva 用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。这标志着第一个基于 mRNA 的流感疫苗在美国获得授权,并且基于一项涉及 40,000 多名参与者的后期临床试验的结果。
规模、影响与风险
据报道,疫苗 预防流感的效果比标准剂量季节性流感 疫苗 高 26.6%。在老年人中,它还表现出比赛诺菲高剂量流感 疫苗 更强的 免疫应答。临床试验期间没有发现重大的新安全问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
该批准立即生效,但由于错过了签约和采购周期,Moderna 预计要到 2027 年底才能产生大量收入。FDA 对 50 岁至 64 岁的成年人给予传统批准,并加速批准 65 岁及以上的成年人,需要更多的研究来证实对老年人群的临床益处。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Moderna wins FDA approval for first mRNA-based flu shot

News Brief
On August 6, 2026, the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved Moderna’s first mRNA-based flu vaccine, mFlusiva, for adults aged 50 and older. This decision followed a lengthy regulatory review and a unanimous recommendation from an independent advisory committee in June 2026. The vaccine is approved for adults aged 50 to 64 with traditional approval and for those 65 and older with accelerated approval, requiring further studies to confirm its clinical benefit in the older population. The approval is based on results from a late-stage clinical trial involving over 40,000 participants, where the vaccine demonstrated 26.6% greater effectiveness against influenza compared to standard-dose seasonal flu vaccines. Additionally, it showed stronger immune responses than Sanofi’s high-dose flu vaccine in older adults. No major safety concerns were identified during trials, but Moderna is not expected to generate significant revenue until late 2027 due to missed contracting cycles. The approval expands Moderna’s portfolio beyond its COVID-19 vaccines and supports its strategy of developing mRNA-based vaccines for multiple infectious diseases.
Original source text (English)
Close Menu Facebook X (Twitter) Instagram Facebook X (Twitter) YouTube Subscribe Thursday, August 6 HOME BUSINESS STARTUPS TECHNOLOGY INTERVIEWS OPINION IMMIGRATION AB PODCASTS AB EVENTS Home » Moderna wins FDA approval for first mRNA-based flu shot Moderna wins FDA approval for first mRNA-based flu shot By Jayujyoti MullickAugust 6, 20263 Mins Read Share Facebook Twitter LinkedIn Pinterest Email The U.S. Food and Drug Administration has approved Moderna’s mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine, making it the first messenger RNA flu shot authorized for use in the United States and marking another milestone for a technology that gained prominence during the COVID-19 pandemic. The vaccine, marketed as mFlusiva, is approved for adults 50 years and older. The FDA granted traditional approval for adults aged 50 to 64 and accelerated approval for those 65 and older, with Moderna required to conduct additional studies to confirm clinical benefit in the older population. The decision follows results from a late-stage clinical trial involving more than 40,000 participants, in which Moderna said the vaccine was 26.6% more effective at preventing influenza than a standard-dose seasonal flu vaccine. The company also reported stronger immune responses than Sanofi’s high-dose flu vaccine among older adults. READ: FDA says US food supply remains safe despite rise in cyclosporiasis cases (July 29, 2026) Unlike traditional influenza vaccines, many of which are produced using egg-based manufacturing, mRNA vaccines can be updated more quickly to match circulating virus strains. Researchers have said the technology could allow manufacturers to respond more rapidly if influenza viruses evolve during a season. The approval expands Moderna’s portfolio beyond its COVID-19 vaccines and supports the company’s strategy of developing mRNA-based vaccines for multiple infectious diseases. The FDA’s decision comes after a lengthy regulatory review. Earlier this year, the agency initially declined to proceed with Moderna’s application, citing questions about comparisons with high-dose influenza vaccines used in older adults. Following additional discussions and a unanimous recommendation from an independent advisory committee in June, the agency approved the vaccine. The vaccine’s most commonly reported side effects were temporary injection-site pain, fatigue and fever, consistent with those seen with other mRNA vaccines. No major new safety concerns were identified during clinical trials. Although the approval is effective immediately, Moderna is not expected to generate significant revenue from the vaccine until late 2027 because the company missed key contracting and purchasing cycles for the upcoming flu season. The approval could also support Moderna’s broader pipeline, including a future combination vaccine designed to protect against both influenza and COVID-19. READ: Wockhardt’s Zaynich wins FDA nod for resistant infections (June 1, 2026) Seasonal influenza causes millions of illnesses, hundreds of thousands of hospitalizations and thousands of deaths in the United States each year, according to federal health authorities. Public health officials continue to recommend annual flu vaccination, particularly for older adults and people at increased risk of severe disease. With the FDA’s decision, mRNA technology is now being used against another major respiratory virus, potentially opening the door to faster vaccine development and more flexible responses to future influenza seasons. FDA 对 mFLUSIVA 的批准凸显了 Moderna 在 mRNA 市场的地位

FDA approval of mFLUSIVA highlights Moderna’s position in mRNA market
新闻简报FDA 已批准 Moderna 的 mRNA 季节性流感 疫苗、mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010) 用于 50 岁及以上的成年人。此次批准标志着 Moderna 在 mRNA 疫苗 市场上的一个重要里程碑,因为 mFLUSIVA 是第一个也是唯一一个独立的 mRNA 季节性流感 疫苗。此次批准基于一项涉及 40,000 多名参与者的关键 III 期试验的数据,该试验证明 疫苗 的疗效比活性对照药高 26.6%。mFLUSIVA 的生产时间较短,传统 疫苗 需要 2 至 3 个月,而不是 6 个月,因此可以更好地匹配季节期间流行的流感病毒株。Moderna 必须解决 FDA 对比较药 疫苗 的选择提出的担忧,并提出修订后的监管途径,包括对 50-64 岁成年人的全面批准,以及对 65 岁及以上老年人的加速批准。疫苗 预计将在 2026-2027 年流感季节上市,随着其使用量的增加,可能会减少现有 疫苗 的市场份额。
查看新闻五要素
时间
FDA 于 2026 年 8 月 10 日批准了 mFLUSIVA。该批准得到了对 40,000 多名 50 岁及以上成年人进行的关键 III 期试验的数据的支持。
地点与对象
该批准适用于全球,针对 50 岁及以上的成年人进行 季节性流感 预防。
事件
FDA 批准了 Moderna 的 mRNA 季节性流感 疫苗、mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010),用于预防 50 岁及以上成年人的 季节性流感。这一批准是根据 III 期试验的数据得到确认的。
规模、影响与风险
mFLUSIVA 是市场上第一个也是唯一一个 mRNA 独立 季节性流感 疫苗。经评估,其疗效比活性对照药高 26.6%,满足非劣效性、优效性和更高级别优效性的标准。疫苗 的生产时间更短,可以更好地匹配流行菌株。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 的批准有据可查,Moderna 预计 疫苗 将在 2026-2027 年流感季节及时为老年人提供。批准过程涉及解决对试验中使用的 疫苗 比较器的担忧,并提出修订后的监管途径。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

FDA approval of mFLUSIVA highlights Moderna’s position in mRNA market

News Brief
The FDA has approved Moderna's mRNA seasonal influenza vaccine, mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010), for adults aged 50 years and older. This approval marks a significant milestone for Moderna in the mRNA vaccine market, as mFLUSIVA is the first and only standalone mRNA seasonal influenza vaccine. The approval was based on data from a pivotal Phase III trial involving over 40,000 participants, which demonstrated the vaccine's 26.6% greater efficacy compared to the active comparator. The shorter production time of mFLUSIVA, which takes two to three months instead of six months for traditional vaccines, allows for a better match to circulating influenza strains during the season. Moderna had to address concerns raised by the FDA regarding the choice of comparator vaccine and proposed a revised regulatory pathway, including full approval for adults aged 50–64 and accelerated approval for those aged 65 and older. The vaccine is expected to be available for the 2026–2027 influenza season, potentially reducing the market share of existing vaccines as its uptake increases.
Original source text (English)

Network Try AlphaSpace Upgrade to Premium FDA approval of mFLUSIVA highlights Moderna’s position in mRNA market FDA approval of mFLUSIVA highlights Moderna’s position in mRNA market · Clinical Trials Arena · Melnikov Dmitriy / Shutterstock.com. GlobalData Healthcare Mon, August 10, 2026 at 11:01 AM CDT 3 min read MRNA The FDA has approved Moderna's messenger RNA (mRNA) seasonal influenza vaccine, mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010), for the prevention of seasonal influenza in adults aged 50 years and older. mFLUSIVA is now the first and only mRNA standalone seasonal influenza vaccine on the market, marking a major advancement for the field. The approval is expected to cement Moderna's position in the seasonal influenza vaccines market. Influenza vaccines produced using mRNA technology will have a shorter production time than seasonal influenza vaccines currently on the market, including egg-based vaccines, cell-based vaccines, and recombinant vaccines. According to Moderna, while traditional seasonal influenza vaccines can take approximately six months from strain selection to launch, its vaccine takes only two to three months to produce. This decrease in production time means that the vaccines can be made closer to the start of influenza season, thereby allowing for a better match to that season's circulating influenza strains. mFLUSIVA's FDA approval was supported by data from a pivotal Phase III trial, which evaluated the safety, reactogenicity, and relative vaccine efficacy of mFLUSIVA versus an active comparator vaccine in over 40,000 adults aged 50 years and older. The most common adverse events were injection site pain, fatigue, headache, and myalgia, and were observed more frequently in the mFLUSIVA group versus the active comparator group. However, the mRNA vaccine was evaluated as being 26.6% more efficacious than the active comparator, which met the criteria for non-inferiority, superiority, and higher-level superiority. Moderna has announced that it expects to have mFLUSIVA available for older adults in time for the 2026–2027 influenza season. mFLUSIVA's path to FDA approval was well documented months earlier, when the FDA reversed course and announced that it would initiate a review of the vaccine one week after the FDA's Center for Biologics Evaluation and Research (CBER) notified Moderna that it would not initiate a review of the seasonal influenza vaccine candidate and issued a Refusal-to-File (RTF) letter, citing concerns over the choice of the vaccine comparator used in the Phase III trial. CBER's RTF letter cited the choice of a licensed, standard-dose, seasonal influenza vaccine used as a vaccine comparator in the trial, rather than a vaccine specifically recommended for older adults such as a high-dose or adjuvanted vaccine, as the reason for refusing to initiate a review of Moderna's biologics license application (BLA) for mRNA-1010. Moderna provided additional data from another Phase III trial comparing mRNA-1010 and a licensed, high-dose, seasonal influenza vaccine, in addition to proposing a revised regulatory pathway for mRNA-1010, including full approval for adults ages 50–64 years, accelerated approval for adults ages 65 years and older, and a post-marketing study in older adults. All of the Phase III data that Moderna reported demonstrated the statistical superiority of mRNA-1010 over the comparator vaccines. Story Continues The FDA approval of mFLUSIVA stakes Moderna's claim in the seasonal influenza vaccines market. As the uptake of mFLUSIVA increases due to its increased effectiveness, the market share of existing influenza vaccines is expected to decrease, with Moderna becoming established as a key player in the seasonal influenza vaccines field. Moderna is already a leader in the mRNA vaccines space. According to GlobalData, of the 27 approved mRNA vaccines globally, the majority are Moderna's assets. "FDA approval of mFLUSIVA highlights Moderna's position in mRNA market" was originally created and published by Clinical Trials Arena, a GlobalData owned brand. Location and population
The approval is relevant globally, targeting adults aged 50 years and older for seasonal influenza prevention.
Event
The FDA approved Moderna's mRNA seasonal influenza vaccine, mFLUSIVA (mRNA-1010), for the prevention of seasonal influenza in adults aged 50 years and older. This approval was confirmed based on data from a Phase III trial.
Scale, impact and risk
mFLUSIVA is the first and only mRNA standalone seasonal influenza vaccine on the market. It was evaluated as 26.6% more efficacious than the active comparator, meeting the criteria for non-inferiority, superiority, and higher-level superiority. The vaccine's production time is shorter, allowing for a better match to circulating strains.
Response, status and uncertainty
The FDA approval was well documented, and Moderna expects the vaccine to be available for older adults in time for the 2026–2027 influenza season. The approval process involved addressing concerns about the vaccine comparator used in the trial and proposing a revised regulatory pathway.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

流感 疫苗 在 2024-2025 年季节提供适度的门诊保护

Influenza Vaccine Provided Moderate Outpatient Protection During 2024-2025 Season
新闻简报A study published in Clinical Infectious Diseases reports that the 2024-2025 seasonal influenza vaccine in the United States provided moderate protection against outpatient influenza illness, reducing medically attended visits by about one-third. Effectiveness varied by strain, with the highest rates against B/Victoria (40%) and A(H1N1)pdm09 (37%), and lower protection against A(H3N2) (27%). Children aged 8 months to 4 years showed the strongest immune response, while adults aged 65 and older saw minimal benefit (VE 4%). The 2024-2025 season was severe, with an estimated 43 to 76 million illnesses, 560,000 to 1.2 million hospitalizations, and 38,000 to 110,000 deaths. Researchers note that even modestly effective vaccines can significantly reduce influenza-associated medical visits and hospitalizations during high-burden seasons.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-08-05;事件数据涉及美国 2024-2025 年流感季节。
地点与对象
美国;分析包括 7 个州的 8 个月及以上在门诊诊所、紧急护理诊所和急诊科寻求护理的患者。
事件
发表在《临床传染病》上的一项研究报告称,2024-2025 季节性流感 疫苗 为门诊患者提供了针对实验室确诊流感疾病的适度保护,其有效性因毒株和年龄组而异。
规模、影响与风险
The 2024-2025 influenza season in the US resulted in an estimated 43 to 76 million illnesses, 560,000 to 1.2 million hospitalizations, and 38,000 to 110,000 deaths. Vaccine effectiveness (VE) against outpatient illness was 33% (95% CI, 24-41), with higher effectiveness against B/Victoria (40%), A(H1N1)pdm09 (37%), and A(H3N2) (27%). VE was highest in children aged 8 months to 4 years (73% against A(H1N1)pdm09 and 41% against A(H3N2)) but low in adults aged 65 years and older (VE 4%; 95% CI, -42 to 35).
应对、状态与不确定性
调查涉及美国流感 VE 网络,医护人员收集呼吸道样本进行 RT-PCR 检测。使用测试阴性设计来估计 VE。研究的局限性包括 疫苗接种 状态的潜在错误分类、未测量的混杂因素、缺乏对 RSV 的系统检测以及缺乏对某些流感病毒株的血清学检测。研究人员强调,即使 疫苗 效果不大,也可以在高负担季节预防大量与流感相关的就诊、住院和死亡。

Influenza Vaccine Provided Moderate Outpatient Protection During 2024-2025 Season

News Brief
A study published in Clinical Infectious Diseases reports that the 2024-2025 seasonal influenza vaccine in the United States provided moderate protection against outpatient influenza illness, reducing medically attended visits by about one-third. Effectiveness varied by strain, with the highest rates against B/Victoria (40%) and A(H1N1)pdm09 (37%), and lower protection against A(H3N2) (27%). Children aged 8 months to 4 years showed the strongest immune response, while adults aged 65 and older saw minimal benefit (VE 4%). The 2024-2025 season was severe, with an estimated 43 to 76 million illnesses, 560,000 to 1.2 million hospitalizations, and 38,000 to 110,000 deaths. Researchers note that even modestly effective vaccines can significantly reduce influenza-associated medical visits and hospitalizations during high-burden seasons.

Menu SECTIONS News Features CME/CE Clinical Tools Conferences Special Collections Multimedia DermDx OrthoDx Clinical Challenge Advisor Forum Fact Sheets Legal Advisor Submit a Manuscript Topics Allergy and Immunology Cardiovascular Cases Dermatology Diabetes Diet and Nutrition Endocrinology Gastroenterology General Medicine Geriatrics Hematology Infectious Disease Legal Advisor Men’s Health Nephrology Neurology Ob/GYN Obesity Oncology Opinion Orthopedics Pain Pediatrics Practice Management Psychiatry Pulmonology Rheumatology Surgery Urology Women’s Health Haymarket Medical Network Cancer Therapy Advisor Clinical Advisor Clinical Pain Advisor Dermatology Advisor Endocrinology Advisor Gastroenterology Advisor Hematology Advisor Infectious Disease Advisor Medical Bag MPR Neurology Advisor Oncology Nurse Advisor Ophthalmology Advisor Optometry Advisor Psychiatry Advisor Pulmonology Advisor Rare Disease Advisor Renal and Urology News Rheumatology Advisor Sleep Wake Advisor The Cardiology Advisor Vaccine Advisor Search for: News CME/CE Features Opinion Advisor Forum Meetings Resources Drug info Part of the News and Features CME/CE Conferences Clinical Tools Special Collections Multimedia Share this article Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on LinkedIn Share by Email Print Influenza Vaccine Provided Moderate Outpatient Protection During 2024-2025 Season Todd Neale, MA | Publish Date August 5, 2026 Seasonal influenza vaccination in 2024-2025 offered moderate outpatient protection, with effectiveness varying by virus strain and age, underscoring the importance of immunization. The 2024-2025 seasonal influenza vaccine in the United States provided moderate protection against outpatient illness, reducing the risk for medically attended influenza visits by approximately one-third, according to study findings published in Clinical Infectious Diseases. The burden of the 2024-2025 influenza season in the US was substantial, with an estimated 43 to 76 million related illnesses, 560,000 to 1.2 million hospitalizations, and 38,000 to 110,000 deaths. Researchers provided updated estimates of vaccine effectiveness (VE) using data from the US Flu VE Network. The analysis included 6793 patients aged 8 months and older who sought care at outpatient health clinics, urgent care clinics, and emergency departments across 7 states for an acute respiratory illness of mild to moderate severity with a symptom duration of 7 days or less. One-third (33%) of patients had received a 2024-2025 influenza vaccine. In seasons such as 2024-25, when the burden of influenza is high, even modestly effective vaccines can still prevent substantial numbers of influenza-associated medical visits, hospitalizations, and deaths. Health care workers collected nasal and oropharyngeal swabs to test for influenza via reverse-transcriptase polymerase chain reaction. Overall, 2016 (30%) patients tested positive, of whom 91% were infected with influenza A strain — 53% with A(H3N2), 42% with A(H1N1)pdm09, and 5% undetermined subtype — and 9% with influenza B. A test-negative design was used to estimate VE, with a rate of 33% (95% CI, 24-41) against influenza illness. The researchers noted VE was highest against the B/Victoria strain (40%), followed by A(H1N1)pdm09 (37%) and A(H3N2; 27%). The researchers reported that VE estimates were higher at younger ages, with the highest rates against A strains observed in children aged 8 months to 4 years —73% against A(H1N1)pdm09 and 41% against A(H3N2). Moreover, VE estimates also tended to be highest for individuals with more recent vaccination prior to illness. Having been vaccinated in the previous influenza season did not significantly influence the findings. The vaccines were also not significantly effective in adults aged 65 years and older (VE, 4%; 95% CI, -42 to 35). Study limitations include possible misclassification of vaccination status, the potential for unmeasured confounding related to study enrollment, and the lack of systematic testing for respiratory syncytial virus infection, of a comparison between full and partial vaccination in young children, and of serologic testing results for cases positive for A(H1N1)pdm09 or B/Victoria. According to the researchers, “In seasons such as 2024-25, when the burden of influenza is high, even modestly effective vaccines can still prevent substantial numbers of influenza-associated medical visits, hospitalizations, and deaths.” Disclosure: Multiple study authors declared affiliations with biotech, pharmaceutical, and/or device companies. Please see the original reference for a full list of disclosures. This article originally appeared on Infectious Disease Advisor References: Chung JR, Price AM, House SL, et al. Location and population
United States; analysis included patients aged 8 months and older seeking care at outpatient clinics, urgent care clinics, and emergency departments across 7 states.
Event
A study published in Clinical Infectious Diseases reported that the 2024-2025 seasonal influenza vaccine provided moderate outpatient protection against laboratory-confirmed influenza illness, with effectiveness varying by strain and age group.
Scale, impact and risk
The 2024-2025 influenza season in the US resulted in an estimated 43 to 76 million illnesses, 560,000 to 1.2 million hospitalizations, and 38,000 to 110,000 deaths. Vaccine effectiveness (VE) against outpatient illness was 33% (95% CI, 24-41), with higher effectiveness against B/Victoria (40%), A(H1N1)pdm09 (37%), and A(H3N2) (27%). VE was highest in children aged 8 months to 4 years (73% against A(H1N1)pdm09 and 41% against A(H3N2)) but low in adults aged 65 years and older (VE 4%; 95% CI, -42 to 35).
Response, status and uncertainty
Investigation involved the US Flu VE Network, with healthcare workers collecting respiratory samples for RT-PCR testing. A test-negative design was used to estimate VE. Study limitations include potential misclassification of vaccination status, unmeasured confounding, lack of systematic testing for RSV, and absence of serologic testing for some influenza strains. The researchers emphasized that even modestly effective vaccines can prevent substantial influenza-associated medical visits, hospitalizations, and deaths during high-burden seasons.

Moderna 的 mFlusiva 获批成为首个基于 mRNA 的流感病毒 疫苗

Moderna’s mFlusiva Approved as First mRNA-Based Influenza Vaccine
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 7 日,美国 FDA 批准了 Moderna 的 mFlusiva®,这是第一个基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗 被授权用于 50 岁及以上成年人。对于 65 岁及以上的个人,批准速度会加快,并取决于上市后研究中验证临床效益。疫苗 针对 2026-2027 年季节推荐的 A 型和 B 型流感毒株——A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09 样、A/Darwin/1415/2025 (H3N2) 样和 B/Pennsylvania/19/2025 样。在一项有 40,303 名参与者参与的 3 期试验中,与标准剂量比较剂相比,mFlusiva 对 RT-PCR 确诊的流感样疾病的 疫苗 相对疗效为 26.6%,满足预先设定的优越性阈值。最常见的不良反应是注射部位疼痛、疲劳、头痛和肌痛。疫苗 预计将在几周内上市。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-07;2026-2027 赛季调整后,未明确报告赛事日期。
地点与对象
美国;50 岁及以上的成年人;65 岁及以上的亚组。
事件
FDA 批准 mFlusiva®,第一个基于 mRNA 的 季节性流感 疫苗,用于针对 疫苗 中包含的甲型和乙型流感亚型的主动免疫。对 65 岁以上成人的批准会加速,并取决于上市后试验验证。
规模、影响与风险
不适用于批准事件;无病例数、死亡或暴露报告。疫苗疗效:整体相对疫苗疗效为26.6%(95%置信区间,16.7–35.4);≥65岁亚组为27.4%。安全性:常见不良反应包括注射部位疼痛、疲劳、头痛、肌痛、关节痛、寒战及腋下肿胀/压痛。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA 批准;疫苗 预计将在未来几周内推出。需要进行上市后试验来确认 ≥65 岁亚组的临床益处。3期试验(NCT06602024、NCT05827978)支持免疫原性和安全性;rVE 满足预先指定的优越性标准。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Moderna’s mFlusiva Approved as First mRNA-Based Influenza Vaccine

News Brief
On August 7, 2026, the U.S. FDA granted approval to Moderna’s mFlusiva®, marking the first mRNA-based seasonal influenza vaccine authorized for adults aged 50 years and older. For individuals 65 and older, the approval is accelerated and hinges on verifying clinical benefit in a postmarketing study. The vaccine targets influenza A and B strains recommended for the 2026–2027 season—A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like, A/Darwin/1415/2025 (H3N2)-like, and B/Pennsylvania/19/2025-like. In a phase 3 trial of 40,303 participants, mFlusiva demonstrated a 26.6% relative vaccine efficacy against RT-PCR–confirmed influenza-like illness compared with a standard-dose comparator, meeting the prespecified superiority threshold. The most frequently reported adverse reactions were injection site pain, fatigue, headache, and myalgia. The vaccine is expected to be available within weeks.
Original source text (English)
Menu SECTIONS News Features CME Clinical Tools Conferences Special Collections Multimedia Diagnosis & Disease Info Clinical Quiz Fact Sheets Topics Axial Spondyloarthritis Connective Tissue Disease Crystal Arthropathies Diseases of Bone and Cartilage General Medicine General Rheumatology Infection in Rheumatic Disease Musculoskeletal Pain Myositis Osteoarthritis Pediatric Rheumatology Practice Management Psoriatic Arthritis Rheumatoid Arthritis Sjögren Syndrome Systemic Lupus Erythematosus Vasculitis Haymarket Medical Network Cancer Therapy Advisor Clinical Advisor Clinical Pain Advisor Dermatology Advisor Endocrinology Advisor Gastroenterology Advisor Hematology Advisor Infectious Disease Advisor Medical Bag MPR Neurology Advisor Oncology Nurse Advisor Ophthalmology Advisor Optometry Advisor Psychiatry Advisor Pulmonology Advisor Rare Disease Advisor Renal and Urology News Rheumatology Advisor Sleep Wake Advisor The Cardiology Advisor Vaccine Advisor Search for: Part of the News and Features CME Conferences Clinical Tools Special Collections Multimedia Share this article Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on LinkedIn Share by Email Print General Medicine Moderna’s mFlusiva Approved as First mRNA-Based Influenza Vaccine Diana Ernst, RPh | Publish Date August 7, 2026 The mRNA-based influenza vaccine is expected to be available in the coming weeks. The Food and Drug Administration (FDA) has approved mFlusiva®, the first mRNA-based influenza vaccine, for adults aged 50 years and older. For persons aged 65 years and older, the vaccine received accelerated approval based on immune responses; continued approval in this age group is contingent upon verification and description of clinical benefit in a confirmatory postmarketing trial. Moderna’s mFlusiva is indicated for active immunization for the prevention of influenza disease caused by influenza virus subtypes A and type B contained in the vaccine. Each 0.38mL dose contains 3 RNAs encoding the hemagglutinin glycoproteins of the following seasonal influenza strains: A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1) pdm09-like virus; A/Michigan/105/2025, an A/Darwin/1415/2025 (H3N2)-like virus; and B/Pennsylvania/19/2025, a B/Pennsylvania/14/2025-like virus. These 3 influenza virus strains align with the FDA recommendations for the 2026-2027 influenza season. The approval was supported by data from a phase 3 trial (ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT06602024) that compared mFlusiva with a standard-dose licensed inactivated seasonal influenza vaccine (Fluarix®) in individuals aged 50 years and older. Study participants were randomly assigned to receive a single intramuscular injection of trivalent mFlusiva or the standard-dose comparator. Relative vaccine efficacy (rVE; primary endpoint) was assessed based on the time to first episode of reverse transcriptase-polymerase chain-reaction (RT-PCR)-confirmed, protocol-defined influenza-like illness (ILI) caused by influenza A or B from day 14 postvaccination up to the end of the influenza season. At a median follow-up of 181 days (range, 1 to 227), RT-PCR-confirmed, protocol-defined ILI occurred in 2% (411/20,179) of mFlusiva recipients and 2.8% (557/20,124) of standard-dose comparator recipients. This corresponded to an rVE of 26.6% (95% CI, 16.7-35.4) in the overall study population, meeting the prespecified criterion for superiority. In a subgroup analysis of individuals aged 65 years and older, mFlusiva demonstrated an rVE of 27.4%. To further evaluate immunogenicity and safety in individuals aged 65 years and older, a second phase 3 study (ClinicalTrials.gov Identifier: NCT05827978) was conducted in which randomized participants to receive either the mRNA influenza vaccine (a quadrivalent formulation; n=1425) or a high-dose inactivated influenza vaccine comparator (Fluzone® High-Dose Quadrivalent; n=1409). Findings demonstrated that the mRNA influenza vaccine met all coprimary immunogenicity endpoints for all vaccine-matched influenza strains. Confirmation of clinical benefit in this demographic will be further assessed in a postmarketing trial, according to Moderna. Regarding safety, the most common adverse reactions reported by age group included: Participants aged 50 to 64 years: injection site pain (68.7%), fatigue (48.1%), headache (41.9%), myalgia (40.6%), arthralgia (31.5%), chills (27.5%), and axillary swelling or tenderness (20.3%). Participants aged 65 years and older: injection site pain (62.9%), fatigue (42.1%), headache (33.7%), myalgia (30.2%), arthralgia (24.1%), chills (18.1%), and axillary swelling or tenderness (14.2%). The mFlusiva vaccine is supplied as an injectable suspension in a single-dose prefilled syringe. It is administered intramuscularly as a single 0.38mL dose. “The approval highlights the tremendous potential of mRNA vaccine technology,” said Ronald G. Nahass, MD, MHCM, FIDSA, President of the Infectious Diseases Society of America. “Unlike conventional manufacturing, the mRNA platform allows for streamlined development and rapid responses to emerging mutations in viruses. Demonstrating the success of mRNA for influenza opens the door to broader applications, accelerating our ability to create safe and targeted vaccines for a wider range of preventable diseases.” The vaccine is expected to be available in the coming weeks.
